Order of work

From the plan (waves; at most four builders and two light agents at a time, one heavy job on the build server at a time):

  1. Wave A, now: S1 to completion; S3, S7 and S9 in parallel (none depends on S1); S13 and this truth pass as light agents.
  2. Wave B: S2, S4 and S5 as S1 lands; S6 as soon as the owner answers; S10.
  3. Wave C: S8, S12, then S11.
  4. Integration: a slice1 branch that builds everything in CI; the signet runs (twice, from a clean start) and the recorded walkthrough.
Rules for this phase
  • Every piece is built against a reviewed specification and reviewed independently before merge; nothing merges with CI red or missing.
  • Builds, tests, mutation runs and clones happen on the build server or in GitHub Actions, never on a personal computer.
  • Work on the build server that predates the history rewrite is replayed onto the rewritten history before pushing; no IP address or local path is ever committed (CI checks this).

Threat model

Who can attack what, and which rule answers each attack.

Threat model · docs/THREAT-MODEL.md

Draft 1, 2026-11-11. Built from section 8 of docs/reviews/spec-review-2.md, edited to match spec/safety-rule.md and spec/signing-round.md draft 3. Not reviewed.

Changes after cross-spec review 1 (docs/reviews/review-cross-spec-1.md, its section 1.2): the rule names now run to H18; 2.2 cites H14 for the host's duty to answer old states; 2.4 cites the exit's clock floor (spec/exit.md 4.5); section 3 gains the exclusions safety-rule.md 11 added with spec/exit.md draft 2 (a wallet left locked past unlock_by, no watch bundle, pinning of keyless anchors, the second-stage breach while locked, a penalty file cut while locked). 2.7 had been updated with spec/storage-encryption.md draft 3.

What this is. For each party that could harm a Gazpacho user: what it can do, which rule is meant to stop it, and what is not defended. It describes what the specifications say. It does not say the defences work. That needs the tests in safety-rule.md section 13 and signing-round.md section 16, none of which exist yet.

Scope. A member and a host running Gazpacho as the specifications and DESIGN.md describe, in 1.0.0: one member per leaf, no sales of receiving room, signet. Notes marked 1.1.0 say what changes when sales and shared leaves arrive.

Rule names: C1 to C13, C8a and H1 to H13 are in safety-rule.md, and H14 to H18 in spec/exit.md section 9 (listed in safety-rule.md section 10); R1 to R7 are its restore rules (section 9); S1 to S5 are the contribution rules of signing-round.md section 3. "SR" is safety-rule.md, "RD" is signing-round.md.

1. What is being protected
Asset Whose Why it matters
Channel balance, and payments in flight Member The money. It must be claimable alone, in time
Factory key, contribution key, channel keys, wallet seed Member, host Theft of these is theft of the money or of the right to sign
Secret nonces Every signer Using one twice reveals the factory key
Held set and exposed set Member Losing the record of what was signed is as bad as losing the transactions
Round records: identifiers, sent bytes, received nonce sets Every signer Protect against being framed; needed as evidence
Restore epoch and guard record Member Stop a restored node from signing again with an old key; detect a stale backup
Funding capital and spare liquidity Host The host funds everything
The host's long-term key and reputation Host It signs terms, proposals and results; proof against it is the valuable kind
Payment preimages Member Releasing one before the payment is irrevocably committed loses the payment
Being able to act in time: online, a true view of the chain, fees Member The deadlines of C5 and the response windows of C4
Privacy: membership, balances, network addresses, links between a person's lobbies Member The host sees much by design; others should not
2. Who can attack, and what stops them

2.1 The host

The host coordinates, co-signs everything, funds, relays every message and keeps members' backups.

It can: choose the participants, their order, the parameters within its terms, and the timing; withhold, delay, reorder or alter any message it relays; pick its nonce after seeing all others; run several rounds; hold seats of its own; withhold results; publish any transaction in a member's exposed set; refuse service; return any backup version it holds; see every payment.

Attack Stopped by Not stopped, or gap
Publish a transaction the member signed but does not hold The exposed set; C3 and C4 draw from it —
Get two trees signed on one funding output C7 (one tree per output, record before the first partial signature); C11; the journal's mandatory sync mark so the record cannot be silently lost Not yet tested: the tests are listed in the specs but not written
The same, after returning an old backup R2 to R5: a restored node uses only new keys, and joins a creation only on a funding output that commits to one Not yet tested (RD test 4b)
Make the member sign an output before it holds its exit from it C6, tiers How this works for channel commitments is not specified yet (SR 4.3)
A stale channel state on an old anchor SR 4.1 rules 1 to 3; H4 The channel engine is not specified yet
An old layer state against an absent member SR 4.1 rule 3; SR section 8 classifies the old state as harmless when its capacity covers the balance, which in 1.0.0 is always The classification is reasoning from the code, not yet checked against the design posts or by test. 1.1.0: a member above its coverage must win a one-step race
A host-only timeout before the member can leave C5, from the scripts —
An unknown spending path or a bad parameter C1, C2, C5, C8 with the full parameter list (RD 4.1) A defect in the parameter list itself
A funding script that is not the participants' aggregate C1 —
A distribution transaction that pays the member less than it is owed C4; C8's bound (lock time at least the end height, share at least capacity) Whether Slice 1 signs one is undecided
Credit on an unconfirmed factory, then the funding spent elsewhere C11; H3 —
Values moved after the ready check C8a; the freeze (terms.md section 8) —
Overlapping rounds C10; H8 —
A withheld cooperative close, published later C7; C13 The member must exit (SR section 11)
A withheld round result SR 4.1 rule 5; RD section 10: the member stays paused, asks again, then exits The only remedy is an exit at the member's cost
Learn the member's key through nonce reuse RD section 8; S1 and S2; S5 after a restore A copy of a running program (accepted)
Frame an honest member Signed nonces over (round, tier, item); unique item numbers; S1, S2, S5 Not yet tested
Escape blame for a round it broke The fault table, when it equivocates under its own key A host with a seat of its own (accepted, RD section 9)
Claim that a member was silent Recorded as the host's claim only Unprovable either way (accepted)
Choose who is in a factory by delaying messages — Denial of service (accepted)
Refuse service Leaving Accepted
See payments, membership and network addresses — Accepted

2.2 A member, seen from the host's side

It can: join for free under a fresh identity; answer late or not at all; send malformed or invalid data; leave alone at any time; route small payments to itself through the host; withhold its partial signature or its acknowledgement.

Attack Stopped by Not stopped, or gap
Spoil a round anonymously H2; the time budget; removal from the next attempt Silence cannot be proven; it costs an attempt, and after a partial-signature step a new funding outpoint (H11)
Withhold its acknowledgement H1: the host does not fund Costs an attempt, as silence does
A free seat that stays silent, then collects at the end date H6, H9; owner open point 2 (a deposit) No cost per seat in 1.0.0
Make the host pay unbounded fees H6, H7 Fee levels are set by the market
Make the host's commitment unrelayable with many small payments H10 The channel engine is not specified yet
An old channel state against the host Channel revocation; H4; H14: the host watches every member's channel output and answers a revoked member commitment by penalty_send_by, signing second-stage penalties as they appear (spec/exit.md 9.1) The channel engine is not specified yet. A host down longer than the shortest revocation window of its members can lose to a breach
An old layer state The newer state wins within one step if the host watches For channel states the duty is H14 (spec/exit.md 9.1); for 1.1.0's timed layers it is not written yet
Leave on day one to disturb the others — Accepted; it costs the host fees

2.3 Other members of the same factory

They can work together and with the host. The host together with all other members is exactly the adversary of SR section 2, so every row of 2.1 applies.

Without the host they cannot sign for a third member, because every output needs every signer below it (MuSig2's unforgeability, BIP 327). They cannot frame a third member, because each contribution is signed by its author [reasoning]. They learn the keys of shared nodes, hence who else is in their branch.

Draft 2 would have shown them each other's channel states in a layer update. Draft 3 marks layer updates not specified and states the requirement that no member learns another's channel state through a round (RD 14.3).

2.4 Chain servers

Public Electrum-style or explorer servers, or the user's own.

They can: hide a transaction; delay a broadcast; refuse a package; report false fee rates; report a stale tip. They cannot invent a confirmation if headers are checked (DESIGN.md section 10).

Attack Stopped by Not stopped, or gap
A fake confirmation of the funding Header checks; two servers compared (Slice 1's chain port) The burial depth is not yet chosen
Hiding the kickoff or an old state Several sources; the maximum safe absence shown to the user (SR section 7) Beyond the safe absence (accepted). 1.1.0: under a day for a member above coverage under a timed layer
Hiding new blocks, a stale tip, or going silent, so the exit starts late The exit's tip estimate has a clock floor: it advances at an assumed two blocks per ten minutes whenever no new block is seen, so hidden blocks, silent servers or offline-by-choice make the exit start early, never late; a claim counts only if a second server reaches it or its headers were checked, so one lying server cannot start an exit either (spec/exit.md 4.5) A full eclipse also stops the member's broadcasts; one broadcast-only route independent of the view's servers is recommended, not guaranteed (spec/exit.md 4.5, open point 15)
Hiding or delaying the member's own broadcast Several servers Package submission was not available on the public Electrum servers found (docs/research/channel-groundwork.md, 1.9)
False fee rates, so the member underpays — Not addressed

2.5 Nostr relays

Listings and host proofs only; not in Slice 1.

They can withhold listings, serve an older signed version of a host's terms, and see who asks. They cannot forge a host's signature. An old terms version leads at worst to a ready that names digests the host does not accept, so the round does not start (owner decision 1). Censorship and metadata are not defended.

2.6 Network observers between member and host

They can see that a device talks to a host and when rounds happen, and drop, delay, replay and reorder packets. They cannot read or forge anything inside the encrypted connection (DESIGN.md section 8).

A replayed round message is answered from saved bytes or refused by the saved identifier (RD sections 3 and 4). Delaying chosen members pushes them out of an attempt (accepted). Membership and timing are visible (not defended).

2.7 A stolen or copied device

Case Stopped by Not stopped
Stolen, locked (or a copy of the data folder taken) Encryption at rest from Slice 1 on (owner decision 13; spec/storage-encryption.md): every file sealed under a storage key that only the wallet passphrase unwraps, through Argon2id at 256 MiB (storage 3, 4). The 24 words need the passphrase typed for them (storage 9) File sizes, times and counts (storage 3.2); a weak passphrase, guessed offline at the cost of one Argon2id per guess (storage 3.3); in 1.0.0, a thief who also has the person's operating-system key store reads the watch bundle (factories, amounts, dates) and can grief by publishing the member's tree path early, but cannot publish a revoked state or move value from its owner (storage 8.7.5, invariant T)
An older copy of the data folder put back (a sync service's history, a system restore, a snapshot) The mark file and the guard catch a journal cut back or rolled back past an anchored batch (storage 6); the key store's counters catch an older watch bundle, penalty file or attempts file (storage 8.7.3); a journal found behind is never started as current, only as a restore under a new epoch The whole folder rolled back together, or together with the key store: not detected locally (storage 6.4); the restore rules then decide (safety-rule.md 9)
Stolen unlocked, or keys extracted — The thief can co-sign with the host and pay the balance out. The safe-to-sign rule trusts its own keys
A copied disk restored elsewhere and run "A restored backup never publishes" (DESIGN.md section 6); C12; R2 to R5 Two devices running one key at the same time
A copy of a running program (a machine snapshot, a fork) holding a secret nonce — Accepted
An old backup returned by the host C12; R2 to R6 The member loses sight of factories joined after the backup, and may not be able to re-derive their keys without the guard record or a later export (R7)
3. Not defended, in one list
  • Refusal of service.
  • A host with a seat of its own escaping blame.
  • Silence and lateness, which cannot be proven.
  • A false view of the chain, or fees out of reach, for longer than the maximum safe absence.
  • Loss of the held set or the exposed set.
  • Two devices running one key.
  • Copies of a running program.
  • A whole data folder put back from an older copy, together with the key store (storage 6.4).
  • Offline guessing of a weak wallet passphrase from a stolen or copied file (storage 3.3).
  • Theft of unlocked keys.
  • Relay rules that obstruct pre-signed transactions.
  • Bugs in the imported tree builder.
  • A reorganisation of the funding after it was buried, or (1.1.0) of a leaf link.
  • The host's view of all payments and of members' network addresses.
  • Withheld results and withheld closes, which force the member to exit at its own cost.
  • Factories joined after an old backup, whose keys may be lost without the guard record (R7).
  • For 1.1.0 members above their coverage: losing a one-step race in a timed layer.
  • For every member below a timed layer: a channel engine that fails to keep an old anchor current.
  • A wallet left locked past unlock_by with a factory balance: the locked watcher publishes the tree path but not the member's commitment, and after H_leaf a dishonest host takes the balance (safety-rule.md 11; spec/exit.md 5.6).
  • A locked wallet with no watch-and-exit bundle ("maximum privacy", no OS key store, or Slice 1): nothing is published while locked.
  • Pinning of a keyless anchor: anyone can spend a tree step's pay-to-anchor output first with a large low-rate child (about 234 sat on a TRUC tree, about 10,000 sat on a version-2 tree), and a member without on-chain coins cannot replace it (spec/exit.md 6.5).
  • A breach while locked, for the payment outputs: penalties against the host's second-stage payment transactions cannot be pre-signed, so a member that stays locked past second_stage_unlock_by loses those outputs (spec/exit.md 4.4).
  • A penalty file cut or rolled back while locked: detected and reported (penalty_file_short), but a host that publishes one of the newest revoked states while the person stays locked past its revocation window takes that channel's balance; not detected if the OS key store is rolled back too (storage-encryption.md 8.7.5).
4. What is not yet known
  • Whether SR section 8's reasoning, that keeping old anchors current removes the loss from an old layer state in 1.0.0, matches the design posts. A named simulator test checks it (SR section 13).
  • Whether the restore rules survive an independent review.
  • The burial depth, the funding and unfinished deadlines, and the confirmation allowance per transaction.
  • Whether the libsecp256k1 session functions, given an unset structure, fail an argument check (and so abort) in the pinned build. Expected, not tested.

Engineering checklist

What every piece of work must satisfy before review, drawn from what reviews found.

Engineering checklist · docs/ENGINEERING-CHECKLIST.md

What every piece of work must satisfy before it is handed to review. It is made from what the reviews of the first pull requests found, so that later work does not repeat those mistakes. Add to it whenever a review finds a new class of problem. Created 2026-11-11.

Before writing code
  1. There is a written specification, and it has been reviewed by someone who did not write it. Code is not started from an unreviewed specification.
  2. The specification says what is stored, in what order, and what happens on a crash at each point.
  3. The tests are listed before the code is written, including the cheating and crash cases.
Safety claims
  1. No comment, header or document claims more than the code does. A sentence such as "never misses" or "cannot happen" needs a test that would fail if it were false. Reviews found a false "never misses" in a header and a check whose name promised more than it proved.
  2. A check that guards a boundary is an allow-list, not a deny-list. Both "this library does no input or output" checks were deny-lists at first, and both were fooled.
  3. Every protection has a negative test: a deliberately wrong version that the test must catch. The wrong version lives in the tests, never in the library.
  4. Every safety rule has a "rule removed" run that must fail, in addition to the test that it works.
Tests
  1. Mutation testing is part of the work, not of the review. The builder breaks the code on purpose, in many small ways, and reports which breakages no test notices. Reviews found 14 such survivors in the core frame and 15 in the wallet keys after the builders had declared them done.
  2. A randomised test compares against an independent model of the rule, written from the specification and not from the code, and compares order and content, not only counts.
  3. Tests do not match loosely. A test that searched output for the letters "prv" failed once in a few hundred runs when those letters appeared in a random address. Match the exact thing.
  4. Fault settings are shown to do something. Two simulator profiles exercised nothing because every node died first. A test of a fault must fail if the fault never happened.
  5. Results are reported with counts, and with what was not tested.
  6. Invariants check the property itself, not a proxy. An invariant that checks something which usually goes with the property (a count, a flag, a state name) passes when the property fails.
  7. Mutation runners kill their children on timeout. A mutant that hangs must be stopped with everything it started; hung mutants once loaded the build server for hours. (Added 2026-11-12)
  8. A new protection gets a cross-check against an independent implementation wherever one can be written. The Python BOLT 8 peer, the independent signature-hash code and the independent parser each paid off. Numbers 27 onwards are added where they belong, not renumbered, so references to earlier items stay valid. (Added 2026-11-12)
State and storage
  1. Save before send, enforced by the runner, never by convention in each engine.
  2. Identifiers are never reused, including across a restart and across a restore from backup.
  3. Anything that makes memory run ahead of storage is fatal for that node: a refused step, a storage failure, storage answering out of order. The node stops and is rebuilt from what was saved.
  4. After a restart, everything saved and not known to be acknowledged is sent again. So every message must be safe to receive twice, and out of order with newer messages.
  5. One-time signing secrets are never stored, and a restored backup must not be able to make one repeat.
  6. A save made early in a start holds back every later send; start records last. In a node's start step, a save comes before every send that follows it, so a save placed early delays all the resends behind it. Records written at start go last.
Code
  1. The core calls nothing outside itself: no files, network, clock, randomness source, environment, printing, or global state. No inline assembly. The isolation check must pass.
  2. Imported files are never edited. A workaround goes in new code and is reported upstream.
  3. Everything from another party or a server is hostile input: bounded, strictly parsed, fuzzed.
  4. Secrets are wiped on every path, never appear in an error message, and are compared in constant time where compared at all.
  5. Arguments that could be confused have distinct types. A yes/no "is mainnet" flag sat where a network value would be natural, and zero meant mainnet.
  6. No warnings, in a normal build and under the memory-error checkers. Uninitialised bytes are checked with a tool that can see them.
Hand-over
  1. Small pull requests. One piece of work, reviewable in one sitting. The first core pull request was over four thousand lines and needed three rounds.
  2. The report says what was decided where the specification was silent, so those decisions can be confirmed or overruled.
  3. Nothing is published by the builder. The branch is fetched, opened as a pull request, reviewed by an agent that did not write it, and merged only when the review and the automated checks pass.

Testing

What runs today and where; then the plan, each item with its milestone.

Testing · docs/TESTING.md

How Gazpacho is shown to work. Two parts, kept apart on purpose:

  • Part 1, what runs today: only what exists, and where it runs.
  • Part 2, planned: the full plan, each item with the milestone that adds it.

Rewritten 2026-11-12 after the gap review, which found that the plan read as if it already ran. Update Part 1 whenever a test is merged, and move an item from Part 2 to Part 1 only when it runs.

Principles

These hold for both parts.

  1. Every safety rule has two tests: one showing the rule stops the attack, and one with the rule removed that must show a loss. The second proves the first can catch the bug it exists for.
  2. The checker is independent of the code it checks. Money is counted from the transactions themselves, not by the module under test.
  3. Failures can be replayed. Anything random is driven by a recorded seed.
  4. Nothing is "done" on a claim. A milestone is finished when its tests pass in the automated checks.

Part 1: what runs today

As of 2026-11-12, on main at 67678bb (after PR 19). Nothing here touches a real network, except where it says so.

On GitHub, on every pull request and every push to `main`

The workflow is .github/workflows/ci.yml: 11 jobs on Ubuntu 24.04, every one required before a merge into main (branch protection, since 2026-11-12). notes/ci.md says what each guards and how to run it locally. In short:

  • gcc 13, two builds: Release, and AddressSanitizer with UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer (GZ_SANITIZE=ON). Each runs ctest, then the scale check. The Release build also runs the wallet break-checks.
  • clang 18, two builds: plain, and ASan with UBSan.
  • clang MemorySanitizer, with secp256k1-zkp and OpenSSL built for it.
  • valgrind on every compiled test of a build without sanitizers.
  • libFuzzer: each fuzz target for 60 seconds (fuzz_noise, fuzz_tlv, fuzz_tx_parse).
  • The imported SuperScalar files are byte for byte what IMPORT.md records.
  • No tracked file names a real address or a local user path.
  • tools/spec-vectors.py runs and its own self-checks pass. It does not compare its output with the specifications' text.
  • The committed handbook matches its Markdown.

The tests that ctest runs:

Test What it checks
reference_tree The imported tree code reproduces the 14 transaction IDs recorded from a real SuperScalar run
action, runner, node The core's action list, the save-before-send runner and the placeholder node
core_isolation, core_isolation_refuses_sneaky_object The compiled core reaches no system call it should not, and the check catches an object that tries
rng, sim The simulator's seeded generator and harness
sim_known_trace One fixed seed gives the trace digest written in the test
sim_seeds 1,000 seeds of 400 steps with every fault at a moderate rate
sim_profile_1 to sim_profile_9 100 seeds of 300 steps under each of nine extreme fault profiles
wallet_vectors, wallet_keys, wallet_rare Wallet keys against the BIP 32, BIP 39 and BIP 86 test vectors, and rare cases
wallet_tool, wallet_tool_faults The gz-wallet-keys tool, including injected faults
wallet_keys_no_io, wallet_no_io_rejects_* The wallet library does no input or output, and the check refuses objects that would
wallet_break_checks (Release build only, label slow) Each protection is damaged in turn in a scratch copy and a test must fail
wallet_tx_vectors, wallet_tx, wallet_tx_faults Wallet transactions (PR 11): the BIP 341 signature-message vectors, serialising and the strict parser, fees and sizes, coin selection, the funding held until release, and injected faults
wallet_tx_fuzz, wallet_tx_fuzz_refuses_flags The parser fuzz loop with its plain driver, and the driver's refusal of a libFuzzer flag as a round count
wallet_tx_no_io, wallet_tx_header_cxx The library does no input or output; its header compiles as strict C++11
wallet_tx_tool, wallet_tx_tool_chain The gz-wallet-tx tool (PRs 12 and 19); with the chain port, against a fake Esplora server on the loopback address only
chain_json, chain_headers, chain_esplora, chain_multi, chain_confirm, chain_model, chain_fuzz The chain view (PR 7): the strict JSON reader, header and Merkle-proof checks, the Esplora backend on recorded answers, the comparing layer, confirmations along linked headers (fabricated signet blocks), a model of the comparing rules, a parser fuzz loop
chain_local_servers, chain_tool_usage, chain_tool_refuses_mainnet_broadcast The libcurl transport against local hostile servers (bad certificates, redirects, slow and huge answers), and the gz-chain tool's usage, exit codes and mainnet refusal. chain_live, against the real signet servers, is opt-in and not run in CI
net_* (including net_fuzz_noise, net_fuzz_tlv and net_gz_net) The transport (PR 10): BOLT 8 and BOLT 1 vectors, connection tests (timeouts, phases, the member check), the wipe test, the frame and TLV fuzz loops, the gz-net tool. net_interop_py, against an independent Python BOLT 8 peer, is opt-in (-DGZ_INTEROP_PY=ON) and not run in CI
tree_shapes 64 8,ps 0 and tree_shapes defects (a workflow step, not ctest) One small scale-check case, and reproductions of two defects in the imported code. These reproductions will start failing when SuperScalar fixes the defects; that is intended, and the step is then changed

The simulator checks four invariants after every step (sim/sim.h): nothing is sent before it is saved; storage keeps what it stored; a confirmation from before a crash releases nothing; the same seed gives the same run. It runs a placeholder node and a test node. No factory can be simulated yet, because no engine is merged.

A gate. Branch protection on main requires all 11 jobs on a branch up to date with main, for administrators too. A pull request with merge conflicts gets no run at all (PR 9 today), so it cannot merge until it is rebased.

On unmerged branches

These run in CI only on their own pull requests, and are not on main:

Branch, pull request Tests CI today
m1a-followup, 9 The core follow-up's tests: runner latches, stepped start, restore from backup, simulator faults No run: the pull request conflicts with main
core-contract-slice1, 21 (stacked on 9) The contract additions: watch kinds, the restore marker, the simulator invariant "secrets never leave" Red: the handbook and the three clang jobs fail
signing-core, 15 (old draft, stacked on 9) The signing engine as it stood when the draft was opened Red

Built and tested only on the build server, not on GitHub: the journal (journal), the lobby engine (lobby), the signing engine's newer state, and the core follow-up's newer commits (SLICE1-WORK.md).

On the build server, by hand

Run by a builder or reviewer when they choose; results are kept in review notes, not in the repository.

  • Mutation testing, with a different script per component (wallet/tests/break-checks.sh, tools/mutate-net.py on transport, and others). Survivor lists are in the review notes listed in reviews/README.md.
  • The full scale check (tree_shapes) at sizes up to 1,000 members (docs/notes/tree-scale-check.md).
  • Long fuzz runs (millions of inputs per decoder), valgrind, gcc -fanalyzer.
  • Comparing python -I tools/spec-vectors.py with the specifications. CI runs the script and its self-checks, but nothing compares its output with the hex printed in the specifications; that was done by hand in the confirmation review.
Not run anywhere today
  • No engine tests: no engine is merged.
  • No regtest: the project runs no Bitcoin node (owner's decision, RECORD.md section 25).
  • No automated broadcast. One signet funding transaction was broadcast by hand with gz-wallet-tx on 2026-11-12; PR 19 fixed the four problems it showed (docs/notes/wallet-tx.md, section 10). Nothing broadcasts in CI.
  • No MuSig2 (BIP 327) vectors.
  • No kept fuzz corpus, no long fuzz runs in CI (60 seconds per target), no coverage report.
  • No Windows or macOS build.
  • No nightly job and no long soak runs.
  • No interoperability test with a real Lightning node.
  • No reproducible build.

Part 2: planned

Each item names the milestone that adds it (PLAN.md). Where a milestone is not decided, it says so.

Levels

2. Standard vectors Official vectors for signatures, group signing (BIP 327), key derivation, recovery phrases, invoices, and Lightning's message and transaction vectors We agree with the standards byte for byte Every push Partly today: wallet keys (BIP 32, 39, 86), BOLT 8 and BOLT 1 (transport), BIP 341 (wallet transactions). BIP 327 with the signing engine (M1); invoices and channel vectors with M2

Simulator A host, many members, a pretend chain and network in one process The whole system under failure and attack Short runs every push (today); long runs nightly Factories in the simulator: M1. Payments: M2. Crash and cheating runs that must pass: M3. Nightly job: not scheduled; wanted before Slice 1 is called done
5. Regtest The real programs with a real Bitcoin node and Electrum server The ports and programs, end to end Core set every push; all scenarios nightly M5 ("a node of our own for controlled-chain tests")
6. Signet and testnet Our public host with scripted users It works on a real network over days Nightly Slice runs by hand from Slice 1; nightly with M6
7. Release candidate Everything above, at full size and length, plus a clean install by hand Fit to hand to outside reviewers Before each release candidate M6
The simulator, as planned

The main safety net. It is possible because the core does no input or output.

  • Schedules: a seeded generator picks operations for every party: join, pay, receive, rotate, close, exit (M1 to M4). Liquidity changes and layer updates come with 1.1.0 (owner decision 9).
  • Network faults: each message may be dropped, delayed, duplicated or reordered. Runs today for the placeholder node.
  • Crashes: each party may be stopped at every point where it saves or sends, and restarted from what it saved. Runs today for the placeholder node.
  • Cheating: scripted dishonest hosts and members try each attack in the threat list (THREAT-MODEL.md): paying a close to themselves, shrinking a balance, publishing an old state, sending a bad partial signature, withholding a result, altering a nonce. M1 for the signing round; M2 and M3 for the rest.
  • After every step, the invariants below are checked.
  • On failure, the seed and schedule are saved so the case becomes a permanent test.
Invariants, as planned

To be checked after every simulator step and at the end of every regtest scenario. Numbers 4 and part of 5 run today (Part 1); the rest need the engines named.

# Invariant Added by
1 Money is conserved across every state change M1 (creation), M2 (payments)
2 Every member can leave alone with at least what they are owed M3
3 A party signs two different transactions that spend the same output only where the safe-to-sign rule allows it (spec/safety-rule.md C7): never two trees on one funding output; two cooperative closes only if each pays it at least what it is owed; one channel state number only for the same state on each of a channel's live anchors, with one revocation secret (section 4.1 rule 1). It never sends two different nonces or partial signatures for one round and item M1 (rounds), M2 (channel states)
4 Nothing is sent before it is saved Runs today
5 No secret nonce survives a restart or is used twice M1 (the restart half runs today as "a confirmation from before a crash releases nothing")
6 No party's software publishes a state it has cancelled M2
7 Every payment deadline is later than the time needed to exit M2 and M3
8 The host's funding is never signed before every exit is signed M1
9 An honest party is never named as the cause of a failed signing round M1
Regtest scenarios, as planned (M5)

Each runs through a Bitcoin node and again through an Electrum server. Until a node exists these behaviours are tested in the simulator only.

  • Lobby fills to target; lobby starts at the deadline; lobby misses its minimum.
  • Join, receive, pay inside the factory, pay out through the host's Lightning node, receive from outside.
  • Rotation into a new factory; cooperative close. (Liquidity change and layer update: 1.1.0.)
  • A member exits alone with the host gone, through to spendable coins.
  • The host publishes an old state; a member publishes an old state.
  • Restart of each program in the middle of each operation.
  • A member away for the whole response window.
  • Restore from backup, then recover without publishing.
Signet, as planned
  • One supervised funding broadcast with gz-wallet-tx and gz-chain: done 2026-11-12. What it found is in docs/notes/wallet-tx.md section 10, fixed in PR 19.
  • Slice 1, 2 and 3 runs by their walkthroughs (SLICES.md).
Sizes

Routine runs use 4, 8 and 32 members. Nightly runs use 128 and 256. Release candidates run the target of 512. (Today: the simulator runs a placeholder; the one-process scale check runs up to 1,000 by hand.)

A second compiler (clang) and MemorySanitizer In CI since PR 16
valgrind in CI on main In CI since PR 16
A thread-error checker for anything concurrent When the host serves more than one connection at once (a choice still open, OPEN-QUESTIONS.md Q4)
Coverage reports Not scheduled
A nightly job for long runs Not scheduled; wanted before Slice 1 is called done
One mutation runner and a committed survivors file per component Not scheduled
A CI check that the imported files match core/superscalar/IMPORT.md In CI since PR 16
A CI check that the handbook is current In CI since PR 16
A CI check that the specifications' hex equals what tools/spec-vectors.py prints Not scheduled. The CI job runs the script and its self-checks only
Windows and macOS builds Depends on the platform decision (OPEN-QUESTIONS.md Q3)
Reproducible builds, pinned toolchain Before mainnet
Compiler warnings are errors Always for new code (-Wall -Wextra -Werror, CMakeLists.txt); the chain port's stricter set is the option GZ_WERROR, on in the gcc CI builds, so a newer compiler's new warning does not stop a local build
Before a release candidate (M6)
  • Every invariant has passing tests at all three of simulator, regtest and signet.
  • Every safety rule has its rule-removed test.
  • The long simulator run and the fuzzers have run clean for a day.
  • Nightly scenarios have been green for seven nights.
  • Each engine has had an independent review, and its findings are closed or recorded as accepted.
  • The documents state what is unreviewed.

Outside review then begins; the owner arranges it.

Review ledger

Every review round of every pull request and specification, with verdicts and open findings.

Review ledger · docs/reviews/README.md

Every independent review of a pull request or a specification, one row per piece of work and one line per round. Started 2026-11-12 after the gap review found reviews archived on the wrong branch or not at all; brought up to date the same day (truth pass), after PRs 7, 10, 11 and 12 merged.

Every reviewer is an agent that did not write the work. No review has been posted on GitHub itself; the files here are the record. GitHub's branch protection requires the 11 CI checks but no approving review, so this file is the only place the review rule is kept.

Rule, from PLAN.md ("How work proceeds": fixes, then a second review of what changed, then merge) and SLICE1-WORK.md: no merge while a high or medium finding is open, while the review of the fixes is missing, or while CI is red or missing.

How the merges of 2026-11-12 measure against it. CI was green (all 11 required checks) on the merged head of each of PRs 7, 10, 11 and 12. Every high and medium finding was answered by a fix commit. But the rule was not fully met on paper:

  • PR 7: the last round (round 3) confirmed the fixes by reading the code only. Its reruns finished after the verdict; their results are summarised below and were never judged by the reviewer.
  • PR 10: round 2 confirmed the fixes of round 1 with runs. The builder's later commits answering round 2's own findings (N1 to N6) were not reviewed before the merge; only CI checked them.
  • PRs 11 and 12: round 2 confirmed the fixes of round 1 with runs and asked for one more fix (the libFuzzer job). That fix commit was not reviewed; only CI checked it.
  • The final rounds of PRs 7, 10 and 11 were archived only after the merge, in this update.

Update this file in the same pull request as each new review.

SIPs: the process

SuperScalar Improvement Proposals: how rules and features are proposed and decided.

SuperScalar Improvement Proposals (SIPs) · docs/sips/README.md

How changes to rules and behaviour are requested, discussed and decided. Modelled on the processes Bitcoin (BIPs) and Lightning (bLIPs) use.

SIPs are meant for anyone building SuperScalar factory software, not only Gazpacho. They live in this repository for now and can move to a home of their own.

Why

New joining rules, safety rules and protocol features usually change the code that decides what is safe to sign. Such changes are made in the open, one at a time, with a written record of who agreed and who objected. The record is kept even for proposals that are never shipped.

Categories
Category For Template
Rule A rule a host can set or a member can require: who may join, when a lobby starts, limits, deposits, profiles template-rule.md (worked example: example-rule-request.md)
Protocol Messages, the host's published terms, transaction construction, signing template-general.md
Process How SIPs or releases work template-general.md
Informational Guidance, analysis, recommended settings; changes no behaviour template-general.md
What needs a SIP
  • Anything that changes what a member or host will sign, or refuse to sign.
  • Any change to messages, the host's published terms, or lobby rules.
  • New rule profiles, joining requirements, deposits, reputation schemes.

Bug fixes and changes that cannot affect signing do not need one.

Life of a SIP
Status Meaning
Draft Written up using a template; not yet under discussion
Discussion Open for comment. Reviewers reply with ACK (agree), NACK (object, with reasons) or a request for changes
Approved The maintainers agree it is sound
Rejected The maintainers do not agree it is sound. The reasons stay on record
Shipped Included in a release, with the software and version noted
Approved, not shipped Judged sound, but not included in Gazpacho releases, with the reason given (for example legal or product reasons). Other factory software is free to implement it
Withdrawn The author gave it up

Approval and shipping are separate decisions. A SIP can be approved and never shipped here, and the record says so plainly.

Rules of the process
  • One SIP per file in this folder, named sip-NNNN-short-title.md, numbered in order of submission.
  • Every SIP states what it changes in the safe-to-sign rules, how it could be abused, and how it is tested.
  • A SIP that touches signing is not shipped without an independent review and a test that fails when the rule is removed.
  • The discussion record (who said ACK or NACK, and why) is linked from the SIP and is never deleted.
Open

Who the maintainers are beyond the project owner.

SIPs: rule template

The form for requesting a rule.

SIP-NNNN: Title of the rule · docs/sips/template-rule.md

  • Category: Rule
  • Status: Draft
  • Author:
  • Created:
  • Discussion: (link)
  • Shipped in: (software and version, or "not shipped" with the reason)
The rule in one sentence

"A host may require …" or "A member may refuse to join unless …".

Who sets it and who checks it
Set by Host / member / both
Where it is published Host's terms / member's own conditions / lobby listing
Checked by Host's software / member's software / both
Checked when On joining / at the ready check / at signing / continuously
Settings
Name Type Range Default Meaning
What it needs to know

The information the check depends on (balances, coins, lobby state, chain state) and where each comes from. Say which of it is supplied by the other party and how it is verified.

Effect on what is safe to sign

What a member's or host's software will now sign or refuse. If the rule can never affect signing, say so and why.

Why it is wanted

The problem, with a concrete example.

How it could be abused

By a dishonest host, a dishonest member, many fake members, or an outsider. What limits each.

Who it shuts out

Honest people the rule would exclude, and whether a host with looser rules remains possible.

Privacy

What new information is revealed, and to whom.

Tests

The tests that prove it, including the run with the rule removed that must fail.

Record
Reviewer ACK / NACK Date Notes

SIPs: worked example

A filled-in rule request: the lobby start rule.

Example rule request (not a submitted SIP) · docs/sips/example-rule-request.md

This is a filled-in sample of template-rule.md, to show the level of detail expected. It describes the lobby start rule already planned for Gazpacho.

SIP-XXXX: Lobby start rule with target, minimum and deadline

  • Category: Rule
  • Status: Example only
  • Author: Gazpacho project
  • Created: 2026-11-11
The rule in one sentence

A host may open a lobby that starts at once when it reaches a target size, starts at a deadline if it has reached a minimum size, and is cancelled at the deadline otherwise.

Who sets it and who checks it
Set by Host
Where it is published Lobby listing and host's terms
Checked by Host's software starts the lobby; each member's software checks the size against the member's own conditions
Checked when Continuously while the lobby is open, and again at signing
Settings
Name Type Range Default Meaning
target_members count 2 to the host's maximum 50 Reaching this starts the lobby immediately
minimum_members count 2 to target_members 30 Fewer than this at the deadline and the lobby does not start
deadline time 3 hours after opening When the lobby starts or is cancelled
on_shortfall choice cancel, or carry members over to the host's next lobby cancel What happens below the minimum
What it needs to know
  • How many members are seated and whose software is connected and ready. The host knows this directly; a member is told by the host and cannot verify the count until signing.
  • Each member's own minimum, held by that member's software.
Effect on what is safe to sign

At signing, a member's software counts the members in the tree it is asked to sign. It refuses if the count is below the host's published minimum or below the member's own. So a host cannot start a smaller factory than promised without every member's software noticing.

Why it is wanted

A factory's on-chain cost is shared among its members, so a host needs enough of them to make it worthwhile, and members want to know they are not joining an empty factory. A deadline lets everyone plan.

How it could be abused
  • A host inflates the count to attract members. Limit: the true count is visible in the tree at signing, and members' software refuses a short tree.
  • Fake members fill a lobby to trigger an early start and then do not sign. Limit: non-signers are removed and the round reruns; if the count falls below the minimum the lobby does not start. This wastes time, not money.
  • A member holds a seat and never readies. Limit: only connected, ready members count toward the start.
Who it shuts out

Nobody. A host that wants no minimum sets it to 2.

Privacy

The target, minimum and deadline are public in the listing. The live count is given by the host on request.

Tests
  • Lobby reaches the target before the deadline: starts immediately.
  • Lobby is between minimum and target at the deadline: starts.
  • Lobby is below the minimum at the deadline: cancelled, or carried over, as set.
  • Members drop during signing and the count falls below the minimum: does not start.
  • With the member-side count check removed, a host starting a short tree goes undetected; the test for this must then fail.

SIPs: general template

The form for protocol, process and informational proposals.

SIP-NNNN: Title · docs/sips/template-general.md

  • Category: Protocol / Process / Informational
  • Status: Draft
  • Author:
  • Created:
  • Discussion: (link)
  • Shipped in: (version, or "not shipped" with the reason)
Summary

Two or three sentences: what changes and for whom.

Motivation

The problem this solves, with a concrete example.

Specification

Exactly what changes: messages, terms fields, lobby behaviour, checks.

Effect on what is safe to sign

Which checks are added, removed or altered. What a member's app and a host's app will now sign or refuse.

How it could be abused

By a dishonest host, a dishonest member, many fake members, or an outsider. What limits each.

Privacy

What new information is revealed, and to whom.

Compatibility

What happens between software that has this change and software that does not.

Tests

The tests that prove it, including the run with the rule removed that must fail.

Record
Reviewer ACK / NACK Date Notes

Open questions

What is still undecided, and who settles it.

Open questions · docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md

Kept current. Rewritten 2026-11-12 after the gap review; the owner's answers to questions 6 recorded the same day (section 0). Choices made on the owner's behalf, which the owner may overrule, are listed in DECISIONS-WITHOUT-OWNER.md.

What was removed, because it is answered:

  • The threat model, the host terms record and the message specification now exist (THREAT-MODEL.md, spec/terms.md, spec/messages.md). The two first specifications have had three reviews and a confirmation (reviews/README.md).
  • Timed levels in 1.0.0 (old O1): none. 1.0.0 trees are advanceless (owner decision 9, ux/decisions.md).
  • The on-chain reserve against timed levels (old O2): not needed, since there are no timed levels in 1.0.0.
  • Seat cost (old O5): free in 1.0.0, then a proof, then per-lobby options (owner decision 10).
  • The precise safety rule and the signing round specification (old 5 and 6): spec/safety-rule.md and spec/signing-round.md.
  • Raising the 256-member limit (old 5c): SuperScalar accepted it; it is tracked in handoff/README.md.

Numbering is new. A document citing an old number ("item 18", "O1") means the list as it stood before this date.

0. Questions 6: the owner's answers (2026-11-12)

questions-6-blocking.html asked only what blocked the first factory: nine questions, each with a recommendation. The owner's answers, with the owner's notes in short and what each leaves to do:

Q Question Answer Decided? What it still leaves

2. Slice 1 inputs not yet written

# What is missing Why Slice 1 needs it Where it belongs

Decided (questions 6, Q3, A), the small-lobby values: fewest members 3; smallest channel 50,000 sat; joining fee at most 2,000 sat; payment fee at most 1,000 msat plus 2,500 per million; factory life at most 8,640 blocks; leaving alone at most 1,008 blocks; these become the Everyday preset, every number visible and editable. Not yet written where it belongs SLICES.md "done when": a member whose conditions are not met is seen to refuse. Without defaults there is nothing to refuse against (confirmation review, S1-5) spec/terms.md section 9, or a short wallet specification

# Question Blocks

Q5 Legal advice before anyone hosts on mainnet (money-transmission and similar law, for the project and for hosts). Prosecutors in the Samourai case treated developers of non-custodial software as money transmitters. The dedication names people convicted or charged in such cases; that belongs on the same list. Gazpacho gives no legal advice (PLAN.md, steps towards real funds) Mainnet hosting
Q6 Proposing bLIP-56 amendments (an odd message type, taproot nonces, no "forget a channel" without evidence). Nothing is posted without the owner's permission The standard path only
Q7 Phones and the signing window. A phone must have the app open during the signing window to move into a new factory. Is a companion device (an always-on machine the member owns, signing within the member's conditions) the answer, and when? Rotation on phones
Q8 Numbers for the lobby: largest lobby, default gathering time, signing timers. Recommended: settle from signet testing. (Slice 1's one-lobby-at-a-time rule is decided, questions 6, Q8; encryption at rest is decided, from the start, owner decision 13) Release defaults

4. For this project to settle (research or build)
  • Lightning's standard taproot channel on an off-chain funding output that moves. The decision rests on secondary sources. Groundwork from the specification: research/channel-groundwork.md. spec/channel.md is not written.
  • Package relay through public chain servers. A forced exit needs a parent and child accepted together. Package submission was not available on the public Electrum servers found (research/channel-groundwork.md 1.9). Without it, Slice 3 needs a node of our own, against the owner's "no node for now".
  • The transport against the message specification. spec/messages.md section 2 requires init, a ping every 15 s of silence and a close at 30 s without a pong. The transport (on main since PR 10) has none of these and cuts any connection idle for 30 s. A lobby gathers for hours. Stream S7 (SLICE1-WORK.md), not started.
  • The core contract lacked four things every Slice 1 specification requires: a secret-tagged randomness request, watch a txid to a depth, watch an outpoint, a restore marker (spec/signing-round.md 12.1). Built in PR 21 (a draft stacked on PR 9, CI red), not merged.
  • The message codec generator. DESIGN.md section 8 plans encoders generated from tables; none exists. Build it before the lobby and round codecs, or drop the plan.
  • Builds beyond Linux. CI has gcc and clang (with ASan, UBSan and MemorySanitizer) on Linux only; no Windows, no macOS (TESTING.md).
  • Long runs. No nightly job; long runs happen only when someone starts them (TESTING.md).
  • The table of tree shapes by size for 1.0.0, where every level is fixed: depth and exit time for each size up to 512.
  • The timelock and deadline numbers for advanceless trees, and the channel's revocation delay, which now sets safe absence (to be fixed in the channel specification).
  • The proof-of-fault scheme in spec/signing-round.md is our own design and needs an expert review.
  • MuSig2 test vectors (BIP 327) are not run anywhere yet; the signing engine needs them.
  • Holding payments open on Eclair, LDK-based nodes and Nostr Wallet Connect.
  • A host proof of funds sized to the lobby it advertises.
  • Whether members can prove past participation, and at what cost to privacy.
  • Acting while the member is away: pre-signed responses, unattended signing, hardware devices (AWAY.md).
  • Adding a member's own coins to their leaf in a live factory: needs a proper review.
  • Waking members who are offline when a factory rotates.
  • The default chain-server list for release. Signet and testnet4 each have two operators now (CHAIN-SERVERS.md); the operators' terms have not been read.
5. Waiting on the SuperScalar repository

Work Gazpacho deliberately does not do itself: it waits, re-imports when the change lands, and checks the result. Every request, its state and the check to run when it lands are in handoff/README.md.

In short, as of 2026-11-12: SuperScalar has merged the advanceless default (our request, their #494), members claiming the end-date payout without the host (#487), rotation off by default (#488, because it hands members' secret keys to the host), eight tree-builder fixes (#486) and others. Still open there: tree fees (#476), the HTLC success-path claim (#474) and the rotation-safety stack (#470 to #473). Gazpacho's import (pinned e657395) predates all of it, so no check has started; the re-import is stream S12 (SLICE1-WORK.md). handoff/README.md has not yet been updated for these merges.

Decisions without the owner

Choices the coordinator or builders made on the owner's behalf, each open to the owner's overruling.

Decisions made without the owner · docs/DECISIONS-WITHOUT-OWNER.md

Every choice the coordinator or a builder made on the owner's behalf that the owner should be able to overrule. Started 2026-11-12 (truth pass). The owner's own decisions are in ux/decisions.md; questions still waiting for an answer are in OPEN-QUESTIONS.md.

To overrule one, say which and what instead. The document named in the "Where" column is then changed, with the owner's answer recorded in ux/decisions.md, and any code that follows it is changed in its own pull request. Nothing here is settled by being written down.

Keep it current. A builder or reviewer who decides something a specification left open adds a row here in the same pull request. A row leaves this file when the owner confirms or overrules it; it moves to "Answered" at the end.

1. Made by the coordinator
# Decision Where it is written What it affects What overruling would change
K1 Chain lag limits: 3 blocks and 30 minutes. Two chain servers that disagree count as "one is lagging" only while the disputed item lies above the lower server's tip, the tips are at most 3 blocks apart on one chain, and the lag has lasted at most 30 minutes; beyond either limit it is a disagreement, and the answer is refused docs/notes/chain-port.md section 12 item 2; GZ_MULTI_LAG_MAX_BLOCKS and GZ_MULTI_LAG_MAX_MS in ports/chain/include/gazpacho/chain_multi.h Every confirmation and coin lookup the wallet makes. Set after round 2 of the review of PR 7, which asked for some bound and named none. Round 3 notes the 3-block limit is the one that binds, and that with three operators one stalled honest server then blocks deep confirmations Other numbers; or the follow-up round 3 proposed (a server far behind counts as no reply)
K2 Slice 1 builds no locked watcher. In Slice 1 a locked wallet watches nothing; no watch-and-exit bundle is written spec/local-api.md 24.1 D17 and the no_bundle reason ("every Slice 1 build"); spec/storage-encryption.md E-D13 ("Not built in Slice 1") Slice 1 members hold no balance, so nothing is at risk on signet; the watcher arrives in 1.0.0. Answers item R11 of the local API's draft-3 review Build the watcher in Slice 1, which adds the bundle, the penalty file and the OS key store to Slice 1's work
K3 A damaged penalty file keeps the watcher running. A short, damaged or unrecognised penalty file is used for what it holds; the watcher goes on, showing watcher_locked with reason penalty_file_short (at_risk). Only a refused bundle stops it (nobody_locked) spec/exit.md and spec/storage-encryption.md, "Changes after cross-spec review 1", item D1 1.0.0 (no watcher in Slice 1). The alternative stops all watching because some answers are missing Stop the watcher when the penalty file is damaged
K4 Invitation-only lobbies, seat removal and standby seats are built in Slice 1. Owner decision 10 names them for 1.0.0; the coordinator brought them into Slice 1 docs/notes/lobby.md decisions 1, 6 and 7, on the lobby branch (build server only) The lobby engine's states and messages.md 6.3 (standby joins are accepted during a ready check and during signing, which 6.3 does not yet allow) Leave them for 1.0.0; Slice 1's lobby becomes simpler
K5 The dedication keeps "people". DEDICATION.md dedicates Gazpacho "to the people who paid for our freedom to hold our own keys" DEDICATION.md line 3 The wording in the repository and, later, in the wallet's About page. Inconsistent today: ux/decisions.md decision 7 quotes the first-run line as "dedicated to the developers who paid …" Use "developers" in DEDICATION.md, or "people" in decision 7's first-run line
K6 Leaving alone always goes ahead, even with a move unfinished. A move or close round left Unfinished past its unfinished deadline is itself a trigger to leave alone; an unfinished move never holds the exit back spec/exit.md section 5.1, trigger round_unfinished; spec/safety-rule.md 4.1 rule 5 Slice 3 and 1.0.0 (Slice 1 members are owed nothing, so the automatic exit does not run) Wait for the move to finish or fail first, at the risk of missing leave_by
K7 Journal record type numbers assigned for the exit and watch records (areas 0x0007 and 0x0008) and for the local API's saved state (new area 0x000d, all even) spec/journal.md "Changes after cross-spec review 1" and section 9.1 Closes Slice 1 input W3 for the type numbers; the field numbers inside each record are still open. Area 0x000d is still to be added to spec/PROTOCOL.md 10.4 Other numbers, before any journal is written with them
K8 The name "T1-long" for the longer test terms (lifetime 144 blocks, wind-down 252), not "T3", which is a vector name in terms.md spec/exit.md section 12, open point 1 Slice 3 tests of rotation with a balance Another name
2. Made by the lobby engine's builder

Written in docs/notes/lobby.md section 2 on the lobby branch, which exists only on the build server today (SLICE1-WORK.md). The note says itself that nothing in it overrides a specification. In short:

  1. Standby seats accept joins during a ready check and during signing (changes messages.md 6.3; K4).
  2. Signing and verifying go through a port; the engine holds no key and has its own SHA-256.
  3. Inputs are whole Lightning messages; "refuse" is a BOLT 1 warning followed by a close notice.
  4. The hand-off to the signing engine: begin and cancel, with the outcome reported back.
  5. A join refused as invalid gets seat state 7 with reason 5 (a reused code); one refused because the lobby is closed, full or limited gets no reason, and the member retries every 1.5 s, at most 30 times per connection.
  6. Invitation-only means the lobby is never named in answers to scope-zero hello (K4).
  7. A host may remove a seat while Open and during a ready check, never after signing begins; a removed key cannot join again (K4).
  8. A ready check is not resumed after a host restart.
  9. The start rule counts seats Ready for the latest values and connected now.
  10. lobby_status goes to a seat only when it changes, at most once a second.
  11. A member resends its saved join and ready when the host has lost them, at most three times per connection.
  12. Slice 1 sizes: at most 32 seats and 48 connections per host; saved ready, start and ready_check at most 256 bytes.
  13. Any error from an engine call stops the node, which is rebuilt from storage.
  14. The 4 × target creation limit counts every seat ever created, rejoins included, across restarts.
  15. A user's unready is kept until the user sets conditions again.
  16. A member that left is told nothing more and does not rejoin that lobby.
3. Made by other builders, in tables of their own

Each table is the list to read; this section says where they are and which entries matter most.

Source What it holds The entries the owner is most likely to want to see
docs/notes/chain-port.md section 12 (13 decisions) The chain view's rules where the documents were silent No majority vote (one well-formed contradiction blocks); the lowest tip is the agreed tip; operators taken from host names unless named; fees fixed at 0.1 sat/vB on test networks, and on mainnet the lowest estimate with a factor-3 disagreement rule and a caller's ceiling; at most 144 headers followed; plain http only for this machine's own names
docs/notes/wallet-tx.md section 5 (12 decisions) The wallet transaction library Funding at output 0 and change at 1, always (which reveals the change); no anti-fee-sniping lock time; largest-first coin selection, not privacy-minded; signing at release only; a rate of 0 refused
spec/transport.md, "Decisions where BOLT 8, BOLT 1 and our documents are silent" (12) The transport Confirmed by both reviews of PR 10, and the owner counted that review (questions 6, Q9). Still open by design: how the host stops one silent peer from stalling it (a step-wise handshake, or capped threads or processes)
spec/storage-encryption.md section 1 (E-D1 to E-D13) The encryption of every file The cryptography choices (Argon2id at 256 MiB and 3 passes, 1 GiB and 4 for exports; XChaCha20-Poly1305; libsodium; a passphrase of at least 15 characters with six generated words offered; the export carries the 24 words only if chosen). They await the owner's yes, and stream S6 waits for it

D1 is answered (owner decision 12)
spec/exit.md section 12 (16 open points with recommendations) Leaving alone and watching H17 (honest host software forces a member out by host_force_by rather than taking a balance by timeout); a restored member never publishes its commitment automatically; fee budget 25% of the recovered amount, 90% once a step is late; UNPROTECTED_MAX 1% of the channel and 10,000 sat; one broadcast-only endpoint. All recommended "adopt"; none confirmed by the owner
The "Changes" tables at the top of spec/safety-rule.md, signing-round.md, messages.md, terms.md, journal.md, local-api.md, storage-encryption.md, exit.md, discovery.md How each review finding was answered Mostly corrections. Choices of policy among them: Slice 1 aborts whole rounds only (signing-round.md, F14); the member's placement in the tree fixed at the import's value 0 and never sent (signing-round.md 4.1, F5); a deterministic last-signer nonce recorded but not adopted (signing-round.md 17); a step timeout of at least 60 s, twice the keep-alive (terms.md 3, F11); limits on seat changes per seat and per connection (messages.md 6, F13); a sync mark after every successful sync, and an invalid entry before it treated as corruption that refuses the start (journal.md, F2)

SuperScalar request ledger

Every request to the SuperScalar repository, its state, and the check to run when it lands.

SuperScalar request ledger · docs/handoff/README.md

Every request Gazpacho has made to the SuperScalar repository, its state from their replies, and what Gazpacho must check when it lands. Started 2026-11-12. The notes themselves are in this folder; this file is the index and the status.

Gazpacho imports SuperScalar's low-level files unchanged (core/superscalar/IMPORT.md) and waits for their changes rather than patching its own copy. When a change lands, the general check is always: re-import, regenerate the reference values (tests/reference/), re-run the scale check, run the case the request names, and update the specifications that cite the old behaviour. The rows below add what is specific.

States: sent (no answer yet), accepted (they will do it), in progress (named in a pull request or round), landed (merged upstream), re-imported, verified (our check passed), refused, superseded.
